=== BUGGY CODE ===
# Definition for singly-linked list.
# class ListNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, next=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.next = next
class Solution:
    def splitListToParts(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> List[Optional[ListNode]]:

        # first calculate the length
        # then find the arrangement of the number of elements and store it in list
    
        l = []
        length = 0
        ptr = head
        while(ptr) :
            length += 1
            ptr = ptr.next

        # possible length of the elements of an node to split
        # first find how much it will be posiibe to split by length // k
        # then calculate the remaining no of elements by length % k
        arrange = []
        maxi = len//k 
        remain = len%k

        for i in range(k) :
            if remain :
                arrange.push(maxi + 1)  
                remain -= 1
            else:
                arrange.push(maxi)

        # now traverse the linked list and split the node 
        j = 0
        ptr = head 
        i = 0
        while(ptr) :
            q = ptr 
            i += 1
            ptr = ptr.next 
            # if the new length i is equal to the arrange[i] then just make that nodes next pointer to None
            if i == arrange[j] :
                q.next = None
                l.push(head)
                head = ptr 
                i = 0
                j += 1

        # if the length is not satisfied then just add none
        for i in range(j,k+1):
            l.push(None)
        return l

Observe the following faulty python code. It contains one or more bugs.
Fix the code and output only the fixed code.

```python
# Definition for singly-linked list.
# class ListNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, next=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.next = next
class Solution:
    def splitListToParts(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> List[Optional[ListNode]]:

        # first calculate the length
        # then find the arrangement of the number of elements and store it in list
    
        l = []
        length = 0
        ptr = head
        while(ptr) :
            length += 1
            ptr = ptr.next

        # possible length of the elements of an node to split
        # first find how much it will be posiibe to split by length // k
        # then calculate the remaining no of elements by length % k
        arrange = []
        maxi = len//k 
        remain = len%k

        for i in range(k) :
            if remain :
                arrange.push(maxi + 1)  
                remain -= 1
            else:
                arrange.push(maxi)

        # now traverse the linked list and split the node 
        j = 0
        ptr = head 
        i = 0
        while(ptr) :
            q = ptr 
            i += 1
            ptr = ptr.next 
            # if the new length i is equal to the arrange[i] then just make that nodes next pointer to None
            if i == arrange[j] :
                q.next = None
                l.push(head)
                head = ptr 
                i = 0
                j += 1

        # if the length is not satisfied then just add none
        for i in range(j,k+1):
            l.push(None)
        return l
```

- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

```
from typing import Optional, List

# Definition for singly-linked list.
# class ListNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, next=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.next = next

class Solution:
    def splitListToParts(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> List[Optional[ListNode]]:
        length = 0
        curr = head
        while curr:
            length += 1
            curr = curr.next

        base = length // k
        extra = length % k

        result = []
        curr = head

        for i in range(k):
            part_size = base + (1 if i < extra else 0)

            if part_size == 0:
                result.append(None)
                continue

            result.append(curr)

            for _ in range(part_size - 1):
                curr = curr.next

            next_part = curr.next
            curr.next = None
            curr = next_part

        return result
```

Let's adjust code:

```
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def splitListToParts(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> List[Optional[ListNode]]:
        if k == 0:
            return []

        length = 0
        curr = head
        while curr:
            length += 1
            curr = curr.next

        base = length // k
        extra = length % k

        result = []
        curr = head

        for i in range(k):
            part_size = base + (1 if i < extra else 0)

            if part_size == 0:
                result.append(None)
                continue

            result.append(curr)

            for _ in range(part_size - 1):
                curr = curr.next

            next_part = curr.next
            curr.next = None
            curr = next_part

        return result
```

Need maybe if length < k, base=0, extra=length, first length parts size 1, rest 0. Good.

Need maybe if length > k, base >=1, extra = length % k. For i < extra, part_size = base+1; else base. Total sum = extra*(base+1)+(k-extra)*base = k*base+extra = length. Good.

Need maybe "for _ in range(part_size - 1):" if part_size is large, but curr is not None. Good.

Need maybe "result.append(curr)" before advancing. If part_size >0, curr is not None. Good.

Need maybe "curr.next = None" detaches. Good.

Need maybe "curr = next_part" after detach. Good.

Need maybe "result" length k. Good.

Let's maybe produce final code as:

```
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def splitListToParts(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> List[Optional[ListNode]]:
        if k == 0:
            return []

        length = 0
        curr = head
        while curr:
            length += 1
            curr = curr.next

        base = length // k
        extra = length % k

        result = []
        curr = head

        for i in range(k):
            part_size = base + (1 if i < extra else 0)

            if part_size == 0:
                result.append(None)
                continue

            result.append(curr)

            for _ in range(part_size - 1):
                curr = curr.next

            next_part = curr.next
            curr.next = None
            curr = next_part

        return result
```

Need maybe if k > length, part_size=1 for first length parts, 0 for rest. Good.

If length=3,k=5, base=0, extra=3, sizes 1,1,1,0,0. Good. If length=0,k=5, base=0, extra=0, sizes 0,0,0,0,0. Good.

Now, if we want to produce a minimal fix preserving original structure, we could do:

```
class Solution:
    def splitListToParts(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> List[Optional[ListNode]]:
        if k == 0:
            return []
        l = []
        length = 0
        ptr = head
        while ptr:
            length += 1
            ptr = ptr.next

        arrange = []
        maxi = length // k
        remain = length % k

        for i in range(k):
            if remain:
                arrange.append(maxi + 1)
                remain -= 1
            else:
                arrange.append(maxi)

        j = 0
        ptr = head
        i = 0
        while ptr:
            q = ptr
            i += 1
            ptr = ptr.next
            if i == arrange[j]:
                q.next = None
                l.append(head)
                head = ptr
                i = 0
                j += 1

        for i in range(j, k):
            l.append(None)
        return l
```

But this still has the zero-size part bug! Let's test length=2,k=3: arrange [1,1,0]. while loop: node1 split j=1, node2 split j=2, ptr=None, loop ends. j=2, range(2,3) append one None. l length 3. If base = 0, then some parts size 1 (extra) and rest 0. The 0 parts occur after all 1 parts because extra = length % k = length (when length < k). So zero-size parts are at the end. The while loop consumes all nodes and j becomes length, then final loop appends remaining zeros. So the zero-size part bug does not manifest for base=0 because zeros are at the end and handled by final loop. If base > 0, no zeros. So original while loop actually works for valid arrangements? There are no zero-size parts before non-zero parts. Thus original algorithm's inability to handle arrange[j]=0 inside while loop doesn't matter because while loop ends before j reaches a zero. Wait, what if base=0 and extra=0 (length=0): while loop not entered, j=0, final loop range(0,k) appends k Nones. Good. Let's ensure final code has no syntax errors:
```
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def splitListToParts(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> List[Optional[ListNode]]:
        if k <= 0:
            return []

        length = 0
        curr = head
        while curr:
            length += 1
            curr = curr.next

        base = length // k
        extra = length % k

        result = []
        curr = head

        for i in range(k):
            part_size = base + (1 if i < extra else 0)

            if part_size == 0:
                result.append(None)
                continue

            result.append(curr)

            for _ in range(part_size - 1):
                curr = curr.next

            next_part = curr.next
            curr.next = None
            curr = next_part

        return result
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def splitListToParts(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> List[Optional[ListNode]]:
        if k <= 0:
            return []

        length = 0
        curr = head
        while curr:
            length += 1
            curr = curr.next

        base = length // k
        extra = length % k

        result = []
        curr = head

        for i in range(k):
            part_size = base + (1 if i < extra else 0)

            if part_size == 0:
                result.append(None)
                continue

            result.append(curr)

            for _ in range(part_size - 1):
                curr = curr.next

            next_part = curr.next
            curr.next = None
            curr = next_part

        return result
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def splitListToParts(self, head: Optional[ListNode], k: int) -> List[Optional[ListNode]]:
        if k <= 0:
            return []

        length = 0
        curr = head
        while curr:
            length += 1
            curr = curr.next

        base = length // k
        extra = length % k

        result = []
        curr = head

        for i in range(k):
            part_size = base + (1 if i < extra else 0)

            if part_size == 0:
                result.append(None)
                continue

            result.append(curr)

            for _ in range(part_size - 1):
                curr = curr.next

            next_part = curr.next
            curr.next = None
            curr = next_part

        return result
